<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, April 14, 2015 
PRESENT: Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Supervisor; Jason E. Whiting, Supervisor; and 
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman were present. Jonathan M. Nez, Supervisor was absent.  
9:02 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Thompson.  
CALL TO THE PUBL IC: Sheriff K.C. Clark read a proclamation for the week of April 13 through April 17, 
2015 to be National Tele Communicator’s Week in Navajo County, AZ, in honor of the men and women whose 
diligence and professionalism keep our cities and citizens safe. Sheriff Clark indicated emergencies can occur at 
any time that requires police, fire or emergency medical services to prot ect life and preserve property. He 
thanked the public safety dispatchers who are the first and most critical contact our citizens have w ith 
emergency services, for exhibiting compassion, understanding and professionalism during the past year. 
 
CONSENT AGENDA : a) Minutes: March 24, 2015 Regular Meeting b) Warrant & Voucher Report over 
$1000 March 2015 c) Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct  #5 and Pinetop -Lakeside #6, March 2015 d) 
Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1; Winslow Precinct #2; Snowflake Precinct #3; Kayenta Precinct 
#4; Show Low Precinct #5; and Pinetop -Lakeside Precinct #6, March 2015 e) Juvenile Probation Report March 
2015 f) Clerk of Superior Court Report March 2015 g) Tax exemptions filed pursuant to A.R.S. §42 -11153 as a 
Request for Redemption of Waiver h) Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount $2,468.16 
APN’s 105-08-049E; 105-54-155; 105-53-350; 105-53-240; and 105 -53-239 i) Navajo County’s Data Sharing 
Agreement between Navajo County and Arizona Public Service j) License Agreement for Arizona Public 
Service Data Files k) Installation of the STOP signs on Black Canyon Lane and Kimball Street at the 
intersections with Black Canyon Road in Heber l) Utility Easement for the Public Works Complex m) 
Reissuance of Stale Dated Checks n) Contracts signed by the County Manger per Board of Supervisors 
Resolution: Contract for Professional Services between Nava jo County and Roman Bitsuie for Consultant 
Resources to Perform Tribal Liaison Services; Contract for Professional Services between Navajo County and 
EcoPlan Associates, Inc. for Various Environmental Surveys; and Contract for Professional Services between  
Navajo County Flood Control District and J.E. Fuller Hydrology and Geomorphology, Inc. for On -Call 
Engineering Services o) Letter of Declaration to renew the Line of Credit for Navajo County p) Congratulatory 
Letter to Pinetop -Lakeside Chamber of Commerce  for receiving the Crown for the Best Cabin Region in the 
United States by Cabin Life’s magazine q) Letter to the Arizona State Forestry in Support for the Town of 
Pinetop-Lakeside Submission for 2015 Arizona Wildland Fire Hazardous Fuels Grant Program r) Thank you 
letters to VFW Post #76; VFW &DFW Post 2364; VFW Post #9907; Ladies Auxiliary Post 9907; American 
Legion Post #60; and VFW & DFW Post #2364 & 60 for attending the Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
meeting on March 24, 2015 item r pulled: Supervisor Whiting made a motion to authorize the Chairwoman to 
sign the items in the consent agenda as presented  with the exception of item r; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
Consideration and possible approval of  Personnel Actions : Sierra Justman  reviewed the 29  item personnel 
action report. Supervisor Williams  made a motion  to approve the personnel actions as presented; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote  unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Thomps on 
commented on the resignation of Trent Larson  and expressed appreciation for the great job Mr. Larson has 
done, especially with the Winslow Levee  project. Supervisor Thompson  wished Mr. Larson well with  his 
future endeavors.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 04-14-2015 
    Page 2 of 5 
 
  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Consideration and possible adoption of a Joint Resolution, Resolution Number 13 -15, with the White 
Mountain Apache Tribal Council in Support of Job Development in Navajo County : Eric Kramer 
presented the item . Mr. Kramer indicated there is a critical need for employment opportunities within 
Navajo County, including the White Mountain Apache  Tribe. Mr. Kramer indicated students and the  
White Mountain Apache Software  company have created a website www.LearingApache.com which 
has created an enthusiastic response. Mr. Kramer gave a demonstration of the web link  and indicated 
this is a r esource for students , teachers and citizens , who want to learn the Apache language. 
Chairwoman Whitesinger  commented on the resolution  and the creation of software jobs . She 
expressed appreciation to Mr. Kramer for his efforts. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve 
the adoption of a Joint Resolution, Resolution Number 13-15, with the White Mountain Apache Tribal 
Council in Support  of Job Development in Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of Proclamation proclaiming April 2015 as “Child Abuse 
Prevention & Awareness Month”: Supervisor Whiting read the proclamation and presented a plaque to 
the Buena Vista Children’s Services, Inc., which is a community based nonprofit organization that offers 
child abuse prevention awareness and services. Supervisor Whiting  spoke of t he importance of 
protecting the children and families in our community,  as they are our future . He indicated the Navajo 
County Attorney’s office will present the 6th Annual Victims' Right Symposium  at the Northland 
Pioneer College auditorium in Snowflake on April 24th. Supervisor Whiting made a motion to approve 
the Proclamation proclaiming April 2015 as “Child Abuse  Prevention & Awareness Month”; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
      ASSESSOR: Presentation regarding the Tax Assessment Process: Cammy Darris, Assessor gave a presentation 
on the tax assessment process for setting property tax rates. She spoke of how the tax assessments and values 
relate to the  jurisdiction budgets. Supervisor Whiting  commended Mrs. Darris for the proficiency and 
professionalism of her office in working and partnering with the communities. 
      SHERIFF: Provide an update on the recently implemented communication system for ja il inmates : Sheriff 
K.C. Clark discussed the item. He indicated the new communication system has been in operation since March 
22nd.  Sheriff Clark reported on the systems functionality and usage, which includes video visitation and e -mail 
in addition to the standard phone service usually offered t o the jail inmates. He indicated the cost is 50 cents a 
minute for the video visitation , which is a lower cost than the national average for video visitation . Ernie 
Garcia, Jail Commander indicated there have not been any complaints about privacy for the inmates and stated 
this will give the inmate’s loved ones easier  access and more times to visit  the inmates, especially for those 
persons who may live further distances from the jail. Supervisor Williams and Supervisor Thompson thanked 
Sheriff Clark and Com mander Garcia for their efforts in making the visitation of inmates, easier and more 
accessible for our constituents. 
FINANCE: Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring April 2015 “Fair Housing Month” 
for Navajo County: Mary Springer discussed the item and read the proclamation  which prohibits discrimination 
in housing. She indicated this proclamation is a requirement for Navajo County to qualify for the Community 
Development Block Grant. Mrs. Springer indicated that this proclamation will be posted with local realtors and 
different venues for the population.  Supervisor Thompson made a  motion to approve the Proclamation

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 04-14-2015 
    Page 3 of 5 
 
declaring April 2015 “Fair Housing Month” for Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams . 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
PUBLIC WORKS: 
a. Consideration and possible approval of a Memorandum of Agreement (MOA) between the Bureau of 
Indian Affairs, Navajo Regional Offi ce and Navajo County: Jeanine Carruthers discussed the MOA and 
stated it serves as a master agreement with the Tribal governments , which  is needed to a uthorize the 
county for road related maintenance activities and the funding there of. She stated the funding will be 
described in the task agreements.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to approve the Memorandum 
of Agreement (MOA) between the Bureau of Indian Affairs, Navajo Regional Office and Navajo 
County; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of a Memorandum of Agreement (MO A) between the Hardrock 
Chapter and Navajo County for the street Network Enhancement Project : Homero Vela discussed the 
item. Mr. Vela indicated this is a partnership with the Hardrock Chapter. He stated the University of 
Arizona will prepare the road data in a GIS format, to serve the chapter for rural addressing. Supervisor 
Thompson made a motion  to approve the Memorandum of Agreement (MOA) between the Hardrock 
Chapter and Navajo County for the street Network Enhancement Project; mot ion seconded by 
Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
c. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B15 -03-003 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt 
Products to Western emulsions, Inc. and Hawker and Evans Asphalt Company, Inc. effe ctive April 23, 
2015: Jeanine Carruthers discussed the item, which is an annual contract . Supervisor Thompson made 
a motion  to approve C ontract #B15-03-003 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt Products to 
Western emulsions, Inc. and Hawker and Evans Asphalt Company, Inc. effective April 23, 2015; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
d. Consideration and possible approval to accept 0.12 miles of FS -504k, East of the intersection of FS-504 
and FS-504k, into Navajo County R oadway Maintenance System: Homero Vela discussed the item  and 
stated this road serves the public including the Salvation Army camp . Supervisor Williams made a 
motion to approve to accept 0.12 miles of FS-504k, East of the intersection of FS-504 and FS-504k, into 
Navajo County Roa dway Maintenance System; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting . Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. 
e. Consideration and possible approval to enter into an agreement with the Salvation Army for the Double 
Chip Seal of FS -504 and FS -504k for appr oximately 1.62 miles : Homero Vela discussed the item. 
Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve to enter into an agreement with the Salvation Army 
for the Double Chip Seal of FS -504 and FS -504k for approximately 1.62 miles; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
      PUBLIC WORKS:  NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session : 
Supervisor Thompson made a motion to enter into the  Navajo County Flood Control District Board of 
Directors Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
a. Consideration and possible approval of the purchase of a drainage easement across Assessor’s Parcel 
Number 411-71-321D for $60,000 as part of regional drainage improvements for Pinetop Country Club:  
Homero Vela discussed the drainage improvements for Pinetop County  Club. Supervisor Williams 
made a motion to approve the purchase of a drainage easement across Assessor’s Parcel Number 411 -
71-321D for $60,000 as part of regional drainage improvements for Pinetop Country Club ; motion 
seconded by  Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible authorization that the Flood Control District enter into a 3 year Joint Funding 
Agreement with USGS for the maintenance of 7 stream -flow gages with an amo unt not to exceed

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 04-14-2015 
    Page 4 of 5 
 
$180,600.00: Homero Vela discussed the item and the importance of partnering w ith the USGS on this 
purchase. Mr. Vela indicated the information that is received from these gaging stations is utilized for 
early flood warning and data collection. He stated that with the e arly flood warning, these gages provide 
us to be able to alert ou r citizens of impending danger. Mr. Vela indicated we can look to the future  
predictions for up to 10 days . Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to approve the authorization that 
the Flood Control District enter into a 3 year Joint Funding Agreement with USGS for t he maintenance 
of 7 stream -flow gages with an amount not to exceed $180,600.00; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Supervisor Thompson made a mo tion to enter into 
regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and Assistant County 
Manager: Supervisor Williams reported he attended the Vietnam Veteran’s Luncheon at VFW Post 9907 in 
Show Low. Supervisor Williams expressed appreciation to Mr. and Mrs. Terry Hill and those who put on  the 
luncheon, which he stated was well attended. He indicated he and Mr. Vela a ttended an  American Legion  
meeting, where he presented a plaque. Supervisor Williams complimented Mr. Vela for his excellent work and 
assistance with the projects within his district . Supervisor Williams  commented on the budget meetings he 
attended. Supervisor Williams thanked Melissa Buckley, Sheila Malone, Debra Chapman and staff for their 
assistance. He indicated he will attend the  Small Counties and CSA  Board of Directors meetings . Supervisor 
Williams reported he will attend a meeting  with the Governor’s C hief of Staff . Supervisor Thompson stated 
he and Mr. Vela had a meeting with the Winslow Mayor regarding the Winslow Levee. Supervisor Thompson 
indicated he m et with the Hopi Tribe’s transportation d epartment regarding roads that need to be improved . 
Supervisor Thompson  reported he met with the Navajo-Hopi Land C ommission regarding transportation  
partnerships for road improvements. Supervisor Whiting invited the members of the Board to attend the Real 
AZ meetings, where the focus is bringing new businesses and jobs to Navajo and Apache County, by marketing 
the region. Supervisor Whiting reported he is pleased to learn the budget process and stated he appreciates the 
Finance staff working with him . Supervisor Whiting commented on the Cedar Hills community c leanup and 
stated he met with Joanne Thompson regarding the efforts in improving their community . Supervisor Whiting 
reported he did a r adio interview with Sheriff K.C. Clark. Chairwoman Whitesinger commented on attending 
budget meetings and extended her gratitude  to the Finance Department in helping her to understand how the  
dollars are spent  in a transparent and responsible manner. Chairwoman Whitesinger  reported she met  with 
Congresswoman Ann Kirkpatrick and thanked her for advocating for our county, especially for her advocacy of 
the Winslow Levee project and also Congresswoman Kirkpatrick’s efforts in restoring the PILT dollars back to 
the counties. Chairwoman Whitesinger  indicated we are the 7th poorest county in the n ation and how  
important it is for the coun ty to get funding resources. Chairwoman Whitesinger reported that the Pinetop-
Chamber of Commerce was given an award for the “Best Cabin Region” within the United States by the Cabin 
and Life Magazine. She stated there will be a Harley Davidson Motorcycle Tour Ride  in September, which will 
be staying in Pinetop/Lakeside.  Chairwoman Whitesinger indicated she met with John Hopkins  in regards to 
their work in Cibecue , in creating  Public Health Initiatives. She stated  she met with  Andrew Kane with the 
White Mountain Apache Tribe and law enforcement officials regarding safety concerns of the tribal areas. She 
indicated Sheriff K.C. Clark has met with various organizations, in finding ways to assist the tribe with public  
safety resources. Chairwomen Whitesinger reported the Sunrise Park Resort closed the day after Easter. Brad 
Carlyon, County Attorney  discussed Assessor Cammy Darris’s presentation regarding  property taxes. Mr. 
Carlyon discussed how the state’s budget is i mpacting the county’s revenues. He stated the state’s mandates for 
payments from the counties to the state , are becoming a problem. Mr. Carlyon stated we need to find a way to 
stop it. Homero Vela, Assistant County Manager  thanked everyone for their compliments, but he stated i t is a 
collective team effort to find solutions to problems . Mr. Vela commented on Mr. Jayne’s  letter to  the

<!-- page 5 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 04-14-2015 
    Page 5 of 5 
 
employee’s on the economic hit  to the county by the State mandates . He indicated it is difficult to give our 
employees salary increases, and stated we are losing employees because the county  cannot offer competitive 
wages for positions. Mr. Vela reported the U.S. Army Corp  of Engineers will tour the Winslow Levee this 
afternoon. He indicated Brigadier General Mark Toy and  Colonel Kimberly M. Colloton, PMP will be in 
attendance Mr. Vela stated if the wind permits, the Army Corp will land their helicopter on the Levee. He stated 
there will be an informal discussion on the status of the Winslow Levee project, to be held with Board members 
and with our partners and friend s of the Winslow Levee  project.  Supervisor Thompson  expressed his 
appreciation to Homero Vela and the Public Works team for their efforts on the  9860 culvert s installation 
project. Supervisor Thompson  reported Supervisor Nez  is running as a Vice -Presidential candidate of the 
Navajo Nation. Supervisor Thompson wished his brother, Supervisor Nez well, in the upcoming Presidential 
election on April 21, 2015.  
 
ADJOURN: At 10:55 a.m. Supervisor Thompson ma de a motion to adjourn, motion seconded Supervisor 
Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
APPROVED:       DATED: April 28, 2015 
 
Jonathan M. Nez, Vice- Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
ATTEST:  
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
